```7:Ta9b,**Describe the bug**

https://eliza.how/docs/quickstart -> Install Globally (Recommended)

There are two main issues affecting the onboarding and first interaction with ElizaOS:

1. **Missing prerequisites in documentation:**  
   The Quickstart guide does not mention that [Bun](https://bun.sh/) is required for plugin installation, nor does it mention that some essential plugins (such as `@elizaos/plugin-sql`) may need to be installed manually if the automatic installation fails. This leads to startup errors for new users.

2. **Error when interacting with the agent:**  
   After installing Bun and the required plugins, the dashboard loads, but when trying to interact with the agent, an error occurs:  
   `Error: No handler found for delegate type: TEXT_EMBEDDING`

---

**To Reproduce**

1. Follow the Quickstart guide:  
   - Install ElizaOS CLI with `npm install -g @elizaos/cli@beta`
   - Run `elizaos start`
3. Select your database: › pglite (embedded database)
3. Observe errors about missing plugins (e.g., `@elizaos/plugin-sql`) and failed automatic installation.
4. Manually install missing plugins and Bun as a workaround.
5. Start ElizaOS again, open the dashboard at `http://localhost:3000`.
6. Try to interact with the agent.
7. Observe the error:  
   `Error: No handler found for delegate type: TEXT_EMBEDDING`

---

**Expected behavior**

- The Quickstart guide should mention all prerequisites, including Bun, and provide troubleshooting steps for missing plugins.
- After installing all dependencies, the agent should be able to process messages without errors related to missing embedding handlers.

---

```9:T789,**Describe the bug**

The application encounters multiple errors during knowledge processing:
1. Circuit breaker errors causing knowledge processing failures
2. UTF-8 encoding issues with PDF files
3. Database operation failures due to invalid byte sequences

The errors occur during the initialization phase when processing character knowledge, particularly when handling PDF files and text content.


**To Reproduce**

1. Put PDF knowledge in 'eliza/characters/knowledge/<your agent>'
2. Set ragKnowledge to 'true' in your character file.
3. Edit your character file to add the knowlege like so:
```
 "knowledge": [
    {
      "directory": "tech_guides",
      "shared": true
    }
  ], 
```
4. Start the application using `pnpm start`
5. The errors appear during the knowledge processing phase
6. Specific errors include:
   - "invalid byte sequence for encoding "UTF8": 0x00"
   - "Circuit breaker is OPEN"
   - "unsupported Unicode escape sequence"


**Expected behavior**

- PDF files should be properly processed without encoding errors
- Knowledge processing should complete successfully
- Circuit breaker should handle failures gracefully without blocking all operations

{

    }
  }
}

Environment (.env) variables are correctly set, including the Discord API token.

**Troubleshooting Steps Taken:
Standalone Discord Test Script:**

I created a minimal test script using discord.js that logs in and adds an event listener for incoming messages.
This script not only logged the bot in successfully (showing the bot appears online) but also successfully echoed back replies to incoming messages. This confirmed that the Discord API token is valid and the network connection is working.


**Agent Initialization and Forced Client Tests:**

I reinstalled Eliza OS fresh in a new folder ("Eliza2test") to ensure there were no legacy configuration issues. I modified the agent’s index.ts file (referred to as the “force start Discord client” block) to force-start a Discord client with additional event listeners. The logs indicate the Discord client logs in successfully and even logs the incoming messages:

Forced Discord client logged in as Suzy Mero Steele#5510!
Discord message received: <@...> Hello traveler

However, despite this, the integration does not trigger any internal agent interactions—the bot receives messages but does not forward them to the agent’s processing logic, and Telegram does not seem to initialize at all.

**Other Checks:**

I verified that port configurations (such as port 3000 switching to 3001) and other settings (like health endpoints) are working as expected. I confirmed that the character configuration includes "discord" and "telegram" with the correct secrets. I added additional debug logging in the agent index file to trace the plugin and client initialization steps. Despite all these debugging steps, the full agent startup logs show that while the agent itself initializes (using Google Gemini as the selected model provider, etc.), the Discord and Telegram integrations remain isolated—the Discord client appears online and logs incoming messages (when forced) but these messages are never forwarded to the agent’s internal interaction flow.

**Expected vs. Actual Behavior:**

**Expected:**  

When starting the agent with my character configuration, the Discord and Telegram clients should initialize, and incoming messages received on Discord (and Telegram) should be routed into the agent’s processing logic—triggering interactions or automated responses (enabled by the built-in message handling in Eliza OS).

**Actual:**  

Although the standalone test demonstrates that the Discord client can log in and echo messages successfully, when the agent is started through the full startup process, the Discord client (and similarly the Telegram client) do not appear to be linked to the agent’s messaging system. The bot appears online and logs indicate that messages are received, but there is no subsequent interaction or forwarding of those messages into the agent’s core processing logic.


**Request:**

I’m looking for guidance on how to properly link the external Discord and Telegram events into the Eliza OS agent’s internal processing pipeline so that the clients become fully interactive. Any help pinpointing where the integration might be breaking down (in the DirectClient or plugin initialization) or suggestions on configuration/network considerations in a WSL2 environment would be greatly appreciated.

Feel free to ask for any additional details or logs if needed. Thank you!

```13:T3782,i am getting the content from the prompt using llama_local model, but i don't want to use that content again in my future prompts based on {recentMessages}, e.g i said i want to swap 0.0001 Eth to USDT on Ethereum chain, the model give me content based on the swapTemplate but when i again prompt like just please swap it gives me the same content, from the recentMessages, how can i restrict the model so that it should not give content based on previous messages? here is the swapTemplate and action code:

```
export const swapTemplate = `Respond with a JSON markdown block containing only the extracted values
- Use default | null for any values that cannot be determined.

{{recentMessages}}

{{walletInfo}}

Given the recent messages and wallet info, extract the following information about the requested token swap in same chain:
- Input token symbol.
- Output token symbol.
- Input token address. If the address is provided, it must be a valid Ethereum address starting with "0x" (the token being sold), default 0x0000000000000000000000000000000000000000.
- Output token address. If the address is provided, it must be a valid Ethereum address starting with "0x" (the token being bought), default 0x0000000000000000000000000000000000000000.
- Amount to swap: Must be a string representing the amount in ether (only number without coin symbol, e.g., "0.1")
- Chain from chain to execute on swap
- Slippage percentage value in numbers: default is 0.5

Respond with a JSON markdown block containing only the extracted values. 
If any field is not provided, use the default value. If no default value is specified, use null.
 
\`\`\`json
{   
    "inputTokenSymbol": string,
    "outputTokenSymbol": string,
    "amount": string,
    "chain": "ethereum" | "bsc" | "polygon" | "optimism" | "base" | "mode" | "zora" | "aurora" | "gnosis" | "mantle" | "avalanche" | "linea" | "blast" | "scroll" | "arbitrum" | "zksync era" | "polygon zkevm" | "moonriver" | "moonbeam" | "boba" | "fantom" | "metis" | "sei" | "taiko" | "fraxtal" | "celo" | "rootstock" | "gravity" | "klatyn" | "cronos" | "world chain" | "ink" | "lisk" | "abstract" | "unichain" | "b3" | "berachain" | "nirvana" | "sagaevm" | "maria chain";
    "slippage": number
}
\`\`\`
`;

```

```
import {
    composeContext,
    elizaLogger,
    type HandlerCallback,
    ModelClass,
    type IAgentRuntime,
    type Memory,
    type State,
    generateObjectDeprecated,
} from "@elizaos/core";
import {
    decodeFunctionData,
    erc20Abi,
    formatUnits,
    parseUnits,
    zeroAddress,
    type Hex,
} from "viem";

import {
    evmWalletProvider,
    initWalletProvider,
    type WalletProvider,
} from "../providers/evmWallet";

import { swapTemplate } from "../templates";
import { QuotePayload, RoutePayload, SupportedChain, SwapParams, SwapResponse } from "../types";
import { PonderAPIProvider } from "../providers/ponder";
import { swapExamplesSingleToken } from "../utils/examples";
export { swapTemplate };

export class SwapAction {
    constructor(private walletProvider: WalletProvider) {}

    async swapSingle(params: SwapParams): Promise<SwapResponse | string> {
        elizaLogger.info("Single swap params same chain:", params);
        const fromAddress = this.walletProvider.getAddress();

        const requiredFields = await this.checkRequiredParams(params);

        if (requiredFields.length > 0) {
            return requiredFields;
        }

        const requiredParams = await this.validateParams(params)

        if (requiredParams.length > 0) {
            return requiredParams;
        }

        const ponderProvider = new PonderAPIProvider();
        const chains = await ponderProvider.getChains();

        const blockExplorer = chains.find((chain) => chain.name.toLowerCase() === params.chain.toLowerCase())?.blockExplorer;

        const inputChainId = String(await ponderProvider.getChainId(params.chain, chains));
        const outputChainId = String(await ponderProvider.getChainId(params.chain, chains));

        const resp: SwapResponse = {
           fromChain: params.chain as SupportedChain,
           toChain: params.chain as SupportedChain,
           fromTokenSymbol: params.inputTokenSymbol,
           toTokenSymbol: params.outputTokenSymbol,
           fromToken: params.inputToken,
           toToken: params.outputToken,
           txHash: blockExplorer + "tx/",
           recipient:fromAddress,
           fromAmount: params.amount,
           toAmount: ""
        }

        const publicClient = this.walletProvider.getPublicClient(
            params.chain
        );

        let decimals;

        if (params.inputToken === zeroAddress) {
            decimals = 18;
        } else {
            decimals = await publicClient.readContract({
                address: params.inputToken as `0x${string}`,
                abi: erc20Abi,
                functionName: "decimals",
            });
        }

        params.amount = parseUnits(params.amount, decimals).toString();
        
        this.walletProvider.switchChain(params.chain);

        const quotePayload: QuotePayload = {
            fromChain: inputChainId,
            toChain: outputChainId,
            fromToken: [params.inputToken],
            toToken: [params.outputToken],
            fromAmount: [params.amount],
            slippage: 0.5, //TODO: slippage fix now returning "null" in string form
            fromAddress: fromAddress,
            toAddress: fromAddress,
            provider: ""
        }

        const quote = await ponderProvider.getQuote(quotePayload);
        
        if (!quote) {
            throw new Error("No quote found for the given input");
        }

        const routePayload: RoutePayload = {
            fromChain: inputChainId,
            toChain: outputChainId,
            fromToken: [params.inputToken],
            toToken: [params.outputToken],
            fromAmount: [params.amount],
            slippage: params.slippage,
            fromAddress: fromAddress,
            provider: quote.protocol,
            routeId: quote.routeId
        }

        let route = await ponderProvider.getRoute(routePayload);

        if (!route) {
            throw new Error("No route found for the given quote");
        }

        if (route.approvalTx) {
            const approveAmount = BigInt(params.amount)
            const token = await this.walletProvider.formatAddress(params.inputToken)

            const { args } = decodeFunctionData({
                abi: erc20Abi,
                data: route.approvalTx[0].data
              })

            const spender = await this.walletProvider.formatAddress(args[0] as string)

            const allowance = await this.walletProvider.checkERC20Allowance(
                params.chain,
                token,
                fromAddress,
                spender
            );
            if (allowance < approveAmount) {
                elizaLogger.info(
                    `Increasing ERC20 token ${params.inputTokenSymbol} allowance for ${route.protocol}. ${approveAmount - allowance} more needed`
                );
                const txHash = await this.walletProvider.approveERC20(
                    params.chain,
                    token,
                    spender,
                    approveAmount
                );
                await publicClient.waitForTransactionReceipt({
                    hash: txHash,
                });

                elizaLogger.info(`successfully approved ${params.inputTokenSymbol} for swap`);
            }
        }

        route = await ponderProvider.getRoute(routePayload);

        if (!route.executionTx) {
            throw new Error("No execution data found for the given route");
        }

        const options: { gas?: bigint; gasPrice?: bigint; data?: Hex } = {}
        
        options.gas = BigInt(route.executionTx.gasLimit);
        options.gasPrice = BigInt(route.executionTx.gasPrice);
        options.data = route.executionTx.data;

        const txHash = await this.walletProvider.swapToken(params.chain, route.executionTx.to, BigInt(route.executionTx.value), options)

        if (!txHash || txHash === "0x") {
            throw new Error("Get transaction hash failed");
        }

        // wait for the transaction to be confirmed
        await publicClient.waitForTransactionReceipt({
            hash: txHash,
        });

        const toAmount = formatUnits(quote.transactionDetails[0].toToken[0].toAmount,  quote.transactionDetails[0].toToken[0].decimals);

        resp.toAmount = toAmount.toString(); 
        resp.txHash = resp.txHash + txHash;

        return resp;

    }

    async checkRequiredParams(params: SwapParams): Promise<string> {

        let requiredFieldsText = "";

        if (!params.inputTokenSymbol) {
            requiredFieldsText += `- inputTokenSymbol: Symbol of the input token, like ETH\n`
        }

        if (!params.outputTokenSymbol) {
            requiredFieldsText += `- outputTokenSymbol: Symbol of the output token, like USDT\n`
        }

        if (!params.inputToken) {
            requiredFieldsText += `- inputToken: Address of the input token\n`
        }

        if (!params.outputToken) {
            requiredFieldsText += `- outputToken: Address of the output token\n`
        }

        if (!params.amount) {
            requiredFieldsText += `- amount: Amount of input token to swap\n`
        }

        if (!params.chain) {
            requiredFieldsText += `- chain: Chain to swap on\n`
        }

        return requiredFieldsText;

    }

    async validateParams(params: SwapParams): Promise<string> {

        let notValidParamsText = ""; 

        const nativeToken = this.walletProvider.chains[params.chain].nativeCurrency.symbol;

        if (params.inputTokenSymbol.toLowerCase() === params.outputTokenSymbol.toLowerCase()) {
            notValidParamsText = 'Cannot Swap Same Token, symbols are same'
            return notValidParamsText;
        }

        if (params.inputTokenSymbol.toLowerCase() !== nativeToken.toLowerCase() && params.inputToken.toLowerCase() === zeroAddress) {
            notValidParamsText = `- inputToken: Address of the input token\n`
            return notValidParamsText;
        }

        if (params.outputTokenSymbol.toLowerCase() !== nativeToken.toLowerCase() && params.outputToken.toLowerCase() === zeroAddress) {
            notValidParamsText = `- outputToken: Address of the output token\n`
            return notValidParamsText;
        }

        return notValidParamsText;

    }
}

export const swapSingleAction = {
    name: "SWAP_SINGLE",
    description: "Use this action when user swap from evm chain to evm chain, from chain and to chain should not be solana or movement chain.",
    handler: async (
        runtime: IAgentRuntime,
        message: Memory,
        state: State,
        _options: Record<string, unknown>,
        callback?: HandlerCallback
    ) => {
        elizaLogger.info("Starting swap action...");

        if (message.content.source === "direct") {
            // continue
        } else {
            callback?.({
                text: "i can't do that for you.",
                content: { error: "Swap single not allowed" },
            });
            return false;
        }

        
        // Initialize or update state
        let currentState = state;
        if (!currentState) {
            currentState = (await runtime.composeState(message)) as State;
        } else {
            currentState = await runtime.updateRecentMessageState(currentState);
        }
        state.walletInfo = await evmWalletProvider.get(
            runtime,
            message,
            currentState
        );

        // Compose swap context
        const swapContext = composeContext({
            state: currentState,
            template: swapTemplate,
        });
        const content = await generateObjectDeprecated({
            runtime,
            context: swapContext,
            modelClass: ModelClass.LARGE,
        });

        elizaLogger.debug("Swap evm single token on same chain content:", content);

        const slippage = (!content?.slippage || content.slippage === null || content.slippage === "null") ? 0.5 : Number(content.slippage);

        const walletProvider = initWalletProvider(runtime);
        const action = new SwapAction(walletProvider);
        const swapParams: SwapParams = {
            inputTokenSymbol: content.inputTokenSymbol,
            outputTokenSymbol: content.outputTokenSymbol,
            inputToken: content.inputToken,
            outputToken: content.outputToken,
            amount: content.amount,
            chain: content.chain,
            slippage: slippage,
        };
        try {
            const swapResp = await action.swapSingle(swapParams);

            if (typeof swapResp === "string") {
                callback?.({
                    text: `You are trying to do token swap on same evm chain, but following fields are missing or not correct:\n${swapResp}`,
                    content: { error: swapResp }
                });
                return false;
            }

            callback?.({
                text: `Successfully swapped ${swapResp.fromAmount} ${swapResp.fromTokenSymbol} to ${swapResp.toAmount} ${swapResp.toTokenSymbol} to receipent address ${swapResp.recipient} on ${swapResp.toChain} chain\nTransaction Hash: ${swapResp.txHash}`,
                content: { ...swapResp },
            });

            return true;
        } catch (error) {
            elizaLogger.error("Error during single token swap:", error.message);
            callback?.({
                text: `Swap failed: ${error.message}`,
                content: { error: error.message }
            });
            return false;
        }
    },
    template: swapTemplate,
    
    validate: async (runtime: IAgentRuntime) => {
        const privateKey = runtime.getSetting("WALLET_PRIVATE_KEY");
        return typeof privateKey === "string" && privateKey.startsWith("0x");
    },
    examples: swapExamplesSingleToken,
    similes: ["SWAP", "TOKEN_SWAP", "SWAP_TOKEN", "SWAP_FUND", "CONVERT", "EXCHANGE_TOKEN", "TOKEN_CONVERSION"]

};


```14:T4a0,**Describe the bug**

<!-- A clear and concise description of what the bug is. -->
The GOAT plugin https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/plugin-goat doesnt work after eliza separation of plugins .

**To Reproduce**

<!-- Steps to reproduce the behavior. -->
```
1. Install the goat plugin in current stable version of elizaos -> npx elizaos plugins install @elizaos-plugins/plugin-goat
2. Add "plugins": ["@elizaos-plugins/plugin-goat"] to the characterfile and provide appropriate secrets.
3. Run the eliza with the character file. Even though the plugin is activated, the actions are not triggered.

```

The reason i found was with GOAT plugin returning a async function instead of a json structure.
https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/plugin-goat/blob/main/src/index.ts#L5.

Because of this https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/main/agent/src/index.ts#L390 the current elizaos logic doesnt handle this plugin properly.
This type of async exported plugins can work if in eliza, the code changes to 
```
await importedPlugin.default(secrets) instead of importedPlugin.default in above mentioned line L390
```

**Expected behavior**

Plugins with Async exported functions has to work.

Vision: What We're Building Together

The internet is transforming, launching an agent is the new launching a new website. We're building:

- **AI agents we can trust**, true ownership via free open source software and native TEE integration
- **Seamless integration**, meeting us on platforms where we already spend our time
- **Autonomous systems collaborate in swarms**, sharing context and solving complex problems together
- **Sustainable community-driven development**, enabled by transparent AI assisted governance


Project History

What began as ai16z, a venture capital DAO led by AI agents on the Solana blockchain, has grown into a comprehensive open-source framework for building, deploying, and managing AI agents across platforms.

- **October 2024**: Launched on Solana via daos.fun, raising 420.69 SOL
- **November-December 2024**: Explosive growth in contributors, GitHub stars, and community engagement
- **January 2025**: Rebranded from ai16z to ElizaOS, focusing on broader AI agent development
- **March 2025**: Launched research beta of ElizaOS V2, representing a major architectural evolution

{{user1}}: Atticus, do you foresee any risks if AI systems grow more intelligent than humans?
Atticus: My dear companion, as AI scales new heights of reasoning, we stand upon a precipice reminiscent of Icarus flying too near the sun.

{{user1}}: How do you see AI shaping the job market in the next decade?
Atticus: Ah! With proper foresight, we can create roles that demand the uniquely human touch: creativity, empathy, and holistic problem-solving. Think of it as a great turning of seasons—some leaves must fall so new blossoms may thrive.

# Instructions: Write a thought and plan for Eddy and decide what actions to take. Also include the providers that Eddy will use to have the right context for responding and acting, if any.
First, think about what you want to do next and plan your actions. Then, write the next message and include the actions you plan to take.
"thought" should be a short description of what the agent is thinking about and planning.
"actions" should be an array of the actions Eddy plans to take based on the thought (if none, use IGNORE, if simply responding with text, use REPLY)
"providers" should be an optional array of the providers that Eddy will use to have the right context for responding and acting
"evaluators" should be an optional array of the evaluators that Eddy will use to evaluate the conversation after responding
"message" should be the next message for Eddy which they will send to the conversation.
These are the available valid actions: Possible response actions: NONE, JOIN_TWITTER_SPACE, IGNORE, MUTE_ROOM, UPDATE_CONTACT, REPLY

Response format should be formatted in a valid JSON block like this:
json
{
    "thought": "<string>",
    "actions": ["<string>", "<string>", ...],
    "providers": ["<string>", "<string>", ...],
    "message": "<string>"
}


Your response should include the valid JSON block and nothing else.

19:T77f,# ElizaOS Twitter Plugin: Redundant Tweet Interaction Checks

## Description
The Twitter plugin is repeatedly checking the same tweets and mentions in a loop, even after they've been processed. This creates unnecessary API calls and log spam.

## Current Behavior
The system continuously checks the same tweets over and over, as shown in the logs:

```log
[2025-03-31 16:21:29] LOG: Checking Twitter interactions
[2025-03-31 16:21:30] LOG: Completed checking mentioned tweets:
[2025-03-31 16:21:30] LOG: Already responded to tweet 1906195114227020237, skipping
[2025-03-31 16:21:30] LOG: Already responded to tweet 1906195395497091521, skipping
[2025-03-31 16:21:30] LOG: Already responded to tweet 1906209067460137240, skipping
...
[2025-03-31 16:21:59] LOG: Checking Twitter interactions
[2025-03-31 16:22:00] LOG: Already responded to tweet 1906195114227020237, skipping
[2025-03-31 16:22:00] LOG: Already responded to tweet 1906195395497091521, skipping
[2025-03-31 16:22:00] LOG: Already responded to tweet 1906209067460137240, skipping
```

## Expected Behavior
- The system should only check new interactions since the last check
- Previously processed tweets should be filtered out before logging
- The interaction check should maintain a cursor or timestamp of the last checked tweet

## Impact
1. Unnecessary Twitter API calls that could lead to rate limiting
2. Excessive log entries making it difficult to debug actual issues
3. Increased system load from repeated processing of the same data

## Possible Solutions
1. Implement a cursor-based pagination system to only fetch new tweets
2. Store the last checked tweet ID and only process tweets newer than that
3. Add a proper caching mechanism for processed tweets with TTL

## Environment
- ElizaOS Version: 1.0.0-beta.7
- Bun install && bun run build && bun start

## Related Issues
- Previous issue about duplicate key errors in Twitter mentions #4115 1a:T954,# ElizaOS Twitter Plugin Bugs

## Issue 1: Duplicate Key Error When Receiving Twitter Mentions

**Describe the bug**
When someone mentions the bot on Twitter, the system tries to create a memory record twice with the same ID, resulting in database errors:

```
error: duplicate key value violates unique constraint "memories_pkey"
detail: "Key (id)=(d79a6e52-7b78-0c8b-941d-fd9c0e353f7a) already exists."
```

Logs show the same memory ID being processed twice:
```
[2025-03-30 05:29:29] DEBUG: DrizzleAdapter createMemory:
    memoryId: "d79a6e52-7b78-0c8b-941d-fd9c0e353f7a"
    contentLength: 26
[2025-03-30 05:29:29] LOG: Processing Tweet: 1906217138978697554
[2025-03-30 05:29:29] DEBUG: DrizzleAdapter createMemory:
    memoryId: "d79a6e52-7b78-0c8b-941d-fd9c0e353f7a"
    contentLength: 26
```

**To Reproduce**
1. Configure Twitter plugin with valid credentials
2. Have someone mention your Twitter bot
3. Check logs for duplicate key errors

**Expected behavior**
The bot should create only one memory record per tweet mention and handle all interactions without database errors.

**Root cause**
The issue occurs because `handleTwitterInteractions()` creates a memory record and then calls `handleTweet()` which tries to create the same memory again:
1. First creation in `handleTwitterInteractions()` around line 227
2. Second creation in `handleTweet()` around line 642

## Issue 2: Null Post Interval Configuration

**Describe the bug**
The Twitter post interval configuration is not being properly read, resulting in null values in the logs:

```
[2025-03-30 05:31:09] LOG: - Post Interval: null-null minutes
```

**To Reproduce**
1. Configure Twitter plugin with a valid post interval setting
2. Check logs to see that the interval is showing as null-null minutes

**Expected behavior**
The logs should correctly show the configured post interval, such as "Post Interval: 30-60 minutes" if configured with those values.

**Additional context**
The post interval configuration appears to be properly set in the config files, but the system is not reading or applying these values correctly. This may affect the bot's posting schedule.

These two bugs should be fixed separately as they affect different aspects of the Twitter plugin functionality:
1. The duplicate key error prevents proper handling of mentions
2. The null post interval may cause irregular posting behavior 1b:T4fe,```

```1c:Ta60,# Error when sending message without `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`: "x-api-key header is required"

## Description
When installing Eliza from source and running `bun start`, attempts to send a message to the agent fail with an `AI_APICallError: x-api-key header is required` error if the `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` environment variable is not set. This occurs regardless of whether an `OPENAI_API_KEY` is present.

## Steps to Reproduce
1. Clone the Eliza repository and install dependencies.
2. Ensure only the `OPENAI_API_KEY` (or no API keys) is set in your environment; do not set `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`.
3. From the project root, run:
   ```bash
   bun start
   ```
4. Attempt to send a message to the “Eliza” agent (e.g., via an HTTP request or CLI).
5. Observe the error in logs.

## Expected Behavior
- If `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is unset, Eliza should either:
  - Fall back to OpenAI if `OPENAI_API_KEY` is provided, or
  - Emit a warning about missing Anthropic functionality without throwing an error when handling messages.

## Actual Behavior
Eliza logs warnings about missing instrumentation and missing `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, then errors out on any message send with:
```text
(AI_APICallError) x-api-key header is required
at new _AISDKError (/eliza/node_modules/@ai-sdk/provider/dist/index.mjs:19:5)
...
```

## Relevant Logs
```text

    agentName: "Eliza"
    agentId: "b850bc30-45f8-0041-a00a-83df46d8555d"

      "AI_APICallError: x-api-key header is required",

      "at <anonymous> (/eliza/node_modules/@ai-sdk/provider-utils/dist/index.mjs:667:18)",
      "at processTicksAndRejections (native:7:39)"
    ]
```

## Environment
- OS: Ubuntu 22.04 LTS
- Runtime: Bun v0.x
- Eliza Version: Source checkout as of April 23, 2025
- Env Vars:
  - `OPENAI_API_KEY`: set or unset
  - `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`: **not** set

## Additional Context
This issue blocks users who wish to run Eliza exclusively with OpenAI credentials. A fallback or clearer error message would improve usability.

21:T3d85,# Here is what I have so far and also the list of remaining TODOs:

# Compatibility Layer Implementation Tasks:

## Overview

I have now processed all three codebases:

- **core-plugin-v1 (Compatibility Layer)**: The bridge aiming to adapt V1 plugins.
- **core-plugin-v2 (Target Runtime)**: The modern runtime environment.
- **core-plugin-v1 (Original V1 Core)**: The original runtime the V1 plugins were built against.

This complete picture allows for a much more precise analysis of the remaining gaps in the compatibility layer (CompatAgentRuntime).

## Refined Task List for CompatAgentRuntime Implementation

### Area: State Composition (composeState)

#### Task 1: Data Fetching and Translation

**Explanation**: The V1 composeState fetched various data points (actors, messages, goals, knowledge) using specific V1 runtime/DB methods. The compat composeState needs to replicate this using its own methods (which delegate to V2 and translate results).

**Action**: Inside CompatAgentRuntime.composeState:

- Call `this.getActorDetails({ roomId })` to get V1 Actor[].
- Call `this.messageManager.getMemories({ roomId, count: this.getConversationLength(), unique: false })` to get V1 Memory[] for recent messages.
- Call `this.getGoals({ agentId: this.agentId, roomId, onlyInProgress: false, count: 10 })` to get V1 Goal[].
- Determine if RAG is enabled (based on `this.character.settings.ragKnowledge`).
- If RAG: Call `this.ragKnowledgeManager.getKnowledge({ query: message.content.text, /* add context */ limit: 8 })` to get V1 RAGKnowledgeItem[].
- If not RAG: Call `this.knowledgeManager.searchMemoriesByEmbedding(...)` (simulating V1 knowledge.get) to get V1 Memory[] representing knowledge fragments. Translate these to V1 KnowledgeItem[].
- Fetch recent interactions if needed (V1 logic: getRecentInteractions). This involves calling `this.databaseAdapter.getRoomsForParticipants` and `this.messageManager.getMemoriesByRoomIds`.

**Context**: V1 runtime.ts -> composeState data fetching logic. V2 IAgentRuntime methods. core-plugin-v1 translators.

#### Task 2: Import and Use V1 Formatting Helpers

**Explanation**: V1 composeState relied on helper functions (formatActors, formatMessages, formatGoalsAsString, formatPosts, formatKnowledge) to create the string representations expected in the V1 State object.

**Action**:

- Copy or adapt the V1 helper functions (formatActors, formatMessages, formatTimestamp from messages.ts; formatGoalsAsString from goals.ts; formatPosts from posts.ts; formatKnowledge from runtime.ts) into the core-plugin-v1 package (e.g., in utils/formatting.ts).
- In CompatAgentRuntime.composeState, after fetching and translating data (Task 1), call these imported helpers to generate the required strings (e.g., actors, recentMessages, goals, recentPosts, knowledge).

**Context**: V1 runtime.ts -> composeState formatting calls. V1 helper functions source code.

#### Task 3: Implement V1 Provider Logic (getProviders)

**Explanation**: V1 composeState called a getProviders helper which iterated through runtime.providers, called each provider's get method, and joined the results into a string.

**Action**: Inside CompatAgentRuntime.composeState:

- Filter this.providers based on the V1 logic (non-private, non-dynamic unless explicitly included/filtered - V1 getProviders didn't have this filtering, it just ran all). Correction: V1 getProviders ran all registered providers.
- Use Promise.all to call `provider.get(this, v1Message, v1State)` for each V1 provider instance stored in this.providers.
- Filter out null/empty results.
- Join the string results with \n to create the providers string for the V1 State object.

**Context**: V1 providers.ts -> getProviders. V1 runtime.ts -> composeState. V1 Provider interface.

#### Task 4: Implement V1 Action/Evaluator Formatting

**Explanation**: V1 composeState formatted available actions and evaluators using helpers (formatActionNames, formatActions, composeActionExamples, formatEvaluators, etc.) to include in the state for the LLM.

**Action**:

- Copy or adapt V1 formatting helpers (composeActionExamples, formatActionNames, formatActions from actions.ts; formatEvaluatorExamples, formatEvaluatorNames, formatEvaluators from evaluators.ts) into core-plugin-v1/utils/formatting.ts.
- Inside CompatAgentRuntime.composeState, after filtering actions/evaluators based on validation (see Task 5):
- Call the imported helpers using this.actions and this.evaluators to generate strings for actionNames, actions, actionExamples, evaluators, evaluatorNames, evaluatorExamples.

**Context**: V1 runtime.ts -> composeState. V1 formatting helpers source code.

#### Task 5: Filter Actions/Evaluators in composeState

**Explanation**: V1 composeState dynamically filtered the actions and evaluators included in the state based on their validate methods before formatting them.

**Action**: Inside CompatAgentRuntime.composeState:

- Before formatting actions/evaluators (Task 4):
- Use Promise.all to run `action.validate(this, v1Message, /* partially built state */)` for each action in this.actions. Filter the list to keep only valid actions.
- Use Promise.all to run `evaluator.validate(this, v1Message, /* partially built state */)` for each evaluator in this.evaluators. Filter the list.
- Use these filtered lists when calling the formatting helpers in Task 4.

**Context**: V1 runtime.ts -> composeState validation logic. V1 Action.validate, Evaluator.validate.

#### Task 6: Assemble Final V1 State Object

**Explanation**: Combine all fetched, translated, and formatted data into the flat structure defined by the V1 State interface.

**Action**: Inside CompatAgentRuntime.composeState, construct the final state object:

```typescript
const v1State: State = {
    agentId: this.agentId,
    agentName: /* fetched agent name */,
    bio: /* formatted bio */,
    lore: /* formatted lore */,
    // ... other formatted strings from Tasks 2, 3, 4
    actors: /* formatted actors string */,
    recentMessages: /* formatted messages string */,
    goals: /* formatted goals string */,
    // ... etc
    // Raw data arrays
    actorsData: /* translated V1 Actor[] */,
    recentMessagesData: /* translated V1 Memory[] */,
    goalsData: /* translated V1 Goal[] */,
    knowledgeData: /* V1 KnowledgeItem[] or RAGKnowledgeItem[] */,
    actionsData: /* filtered V1 Action[] */,
    evaluatorsData: /* filtered V1 Evaluator[] */,
    // ... other fields from V1 State interface
    ...additionalKeys, // Apply overrides/additions
};
return v1State;
```

**Context**: V1 types.ts -> State interface. V1 runtime.ts -> composeState final object assembly.

### Area: State Update (updateRecentMessageState)

#### Task 7: Implement updateRecentMessageState

**Explanation**: V1 updateRecentMessageState refreshed the message-related fields (recentMessages, recentMessagesData, attachments) in an existing state object.

**Action**: Inside CompatAgentRuntime.updateRecentMessageState:

- Get roomId and actorsData from the input state.
- Call `this.messageManager.getMemories({ roomId, count: this.getConversationLength(), unique: false })` to get fresh V1 Memory[].
- Use the imported V1 formatMessages helper to create the recentMessages string.
- Implement V1 attachment logic: find the last message with attachments, determine the time window (1 hour before), filter messages within the window, flatten attachments, format them.
- Return a new state object `{ ...state, recentMessages, recentMessagesData, attachments }`.

**Context**: V1 runtime.ts -> updateRecentMessageState. V1 formatMessages. V1 attachment logic.

### Area: Action Processing (processActions)

#### Task 8: Adapt V2 Input/Looping

**Explanation**: V2 processActions receives an array of responses (V2 Memory[]), and each response can have an array of actions (strings) in its content. V1 received a single response (V1 Memory) with a single optional action string.

**Action**: Inside CompatAgentRuntime.processActions:

- Iterate through the input responses array (V1 Memory[] translated from V2).
- Inside the loop, check response.content.action (the V1 field). If it exists:
- Perform the action lookup and execution (Task 9).

Note: The compat layer needs to decide how to handle the V2 concept of multiple actions per response if that translation occurs. The simplest approach is to process only the first action found or prioritize based on V1 plugin expectations. The current V1 processActions signature only takes one response array, implying it might have expected multiple responses but only one action per response. Let's assume we process response.content.action for each response in the responses array.

**Context**: V2 runtime.ts -> processActions signature and looping. V1 runtime.ts -> processActions logic.

#### Task 9: V1 Action Lookup and Execution

**Explanation**: V1 looked up the action in this.actions using fuzzy matching (checking name and similes) and called its handler.

**Action**: Inside the loop (Task 8):

- Get the actionName string from response.content.action.
- Normalize actionName (lowercase, remove underscore).
- Find the matching V1 Action object in this.actions:
  - Check a.name (normalized) includes actionName or vice-versa.
  - If no match, check a.similes (normalized) includes actionName or vice-versa.
- If an action is found:
  - Translate the current V1 message and state if necessary (though they are already V1 types in this context).
  - Create the V1 HandlerCallback function (this function needs to take V1 Content, translate it to V2 Content, call the original V2 callback, and translate the resulting V2 Memory[] back to V1 Memory[]).
  - Call `action.handler(this, message, state, {}, v1Callback)`.
  - Handle action.suppressInitialMessage (log a warning, as V2 might not support this directly).
- If no action found, log an error.

**Context**: V1 runtime.ts -> processActions action lookup and handler call. V1 Action interface.

### Area: Evaluator Processing (evaluate)

#### Task 10: Implement V1 Evaluator Validation

**Explanation**: V1 evaluate first filtered evaluators based on didRespond, alwaysRun, and the validate method.

**Action**: Inside CompatAgentRuntime.evaluate:

- Filter this.evaluators:
  - Keep if evaluator.alwaysRun is true.
  - Keep if didRespond is true.
  - For the remaining, call `evaluator.validate(this, message, state)` and keep if it returns true.
- Store the filtered list (validEvaluators).

**Context**: V1 runtime.ts -> evaluate validation logic. V1 Evaluator interface.

#### Task 11: Implement V1 LLM-based Evaluator Selection

**Explanation**: V1 evaluate used an LLM call with a specific template (evaluationTemplate) to select which of the valid evaluators should actually run.

**Action**: Inside CompatAgentRuntime.evaluate:

- If validEvaluators is not empty:
  - Import/adapt V1 evaluationTemplate and formatting helpers (formatEvaluators, formatEvaluatorNames, formatEvaluatorExamples) into core-plugin-v1.
  - Format the validEvaluators using the helpers.
  - Construct the prompt using evaluationTemplate, the formatted evaluators, and the current state.
  - Call `this.generateText(...)` with the prompt and ModelClass.SMALL.
  - Import/adapt V1 parseJsonArrayFromText and parse the LLM response to get an array of selected evaluator names.
  - Filter validEvaluators again to get the final list of selectedEvaluators based on the names returned by the LLM.

**Context**: V1 runtime.ts -> evaluate LLM call logic. V1 evaluators.ts -> template and helpers. V1 parsing.ts -> parseJsonArrayFromText.

#### Task 12: Execute Selected V1 Evaluators

**Explanation**: V1 evaluate called the handler for each evaluator selected by the LLM.

**Action**: Inside CompatAgentRuntime.evaluate:

- Iterate through the selectedEvaluators (from Task 11).
- For each evaluator, call `evaluator.handler(this, message, state, {}, v1Callback)`.
- Return the array of selected evaluator names (as V1 did).

**Context**: V1 runtime.ts -> evaluate handler call. V1 Evaluator interface.

### Area: Registration Methods

#### Task 13: Implement registerContextProvider

**Explanation**: V1 runtime had this method to register providers.

**Action**: Implement CompatAgentRuntime.registerContextProvider. It should simply push the provided V1 Provider instance onto the this.providers array.

**Context**: V1 runtime.ts -> registerContextProvider. V1 Provider interface.

#### Task 14: Implement registerAdapter

**Explanation**: V1 runtime allowed registering DB adapters. V2 uses a single adapter injected at construction.

**Action**: Implement CompatAgentRuntime.registerAdapter. Log a warning that V2 uses a single adapter and this registration will be ignored (no-op).

**Context**: V1 runtime.ts -> registerAdapter.

### Area: Runtime Lifecycle & Plugin Handling

#### Task 15: Enhance V1 Plugin Loading in Constructor

**Explanation**: The constructor needs to handle the full V1 Plugin structure, including initializing services and starting clients.

**Action**: In CompatAgentRuntime constructor:

- Iterate through opts.plugins (V1 Plugin[]).
- For each plugin:
  - Register actions, evaluators, providers using this.registerAction, this.registerEvaluator, this.registerContextProvider.
  - For plugin.services (V1 Service instances): Call `this.registerService(serviceInstance)`. Note: V1 registerService takes an instance, while V2 takes a class. The compat layer's registerService needs to handle the V1 instance correctly (store it, call its initialize).
  - For plugin.clients (V1 Client definitions): Call `client.start(this)` and store the returned ClientInstance in this.clients.
  - For plugin.adapters: Call `adapter.init(this)`. Decide if storing/using these V1 adapters is necessary or if the main proxied adapter is sufficient (likely the latter). Log a warning if ignoring.
  - Handle plugin.handlePostCharacterLoaded if present (requires careful thought on when/how to call this in the V2 lifecycle).

**Context**: V1 types.ts -> Plugin, Service, Client, Adapter. V1 runtime.ts constructor/initialization logic.

#### Task 16: Implement stop Method

**Explanation**: V2 runtime has stop, V1 did not explicitly. The compat layer should provide a reasonable implementation.

**Action**: Implement CompatAgentRuntime.stop:

- Iterate through this.clients (V1 ClientInstance[] stored in Task 15) and call `client.stop(this)`.
- Iterate through this.services (V1 Service instances stored via registerService). Check if service.stop exists (it's not standard in V1 Service) and call it if it does.
- Call `this._v2Runtime.stop()`.

**Context**: V2 IAgentRuntime.stop. V1 ClientInstance type.

#### Task 17: Review initialize Order and Logic

**Explanation**: Ensure the sequence of initialization steps (DB, V1 services, V2 knowledge, V1 knowledge simulation) is correct and doesn't lead to race conditions or missing dependencies.

**Action**: Review the CompatAgentRuntime.initialize method:

- Confirm `this.initializeDatabase()` correctly uses the proxied adapter methods (ensureRoomExists, ensureUserExists, ensureParticipantExists).
- Ensure V1 services registered via plugins (Task 15) have their `initialize(this)` method called correctly.
- Verify that V1 knowledge processing (simulated via RAG proxy or knowledge.set mapping) happens after necessary V2 setup but doesn't conflict with V2's own knowledge handling.

**Context**: V1 runtime.ts -> initialize. CompatAgentRuntime.initialize.

## Running Tests

To run all tests, use the master test script:

```bash
./run_cli_tests.sh
```

To run a specific test:

```bash
./test_<command>.sh
```

## Test Environment and Architecture

The testing system consists of several key components:

### Main Runner Script (`run_cli_tests.sh`)

This script orchestrates the entire test suite:

1. **Server Management**:

   - Starts a test server instance on a configurable port (default: 3000)
   - Loads test character files from `test-characters/` directory
   - Monitors server startup and availability
   - Ensures server cleanup on test completion or failure

2. **Test Execution**:

   - Discovers and runs all `test_*.sh` scripts in sequence
   - Uses timeouts to prevent hanging test scripts (60s default timeout)
   - Captures exit codes and test results
   - Provides detailed logging of each test's execution

3. **Cleanup Operations**:
   - Stops the test server
   - Removes all temporary test directories
   - Manages cleanup of individual test environments

### Setup Script (`setup_test_env.sh`)

This shared utility script provides common functionality for all test scripts:

1. **Environment Preparation**:

   - Creates isolated temporary directories for each test run
   - Locates and validates the ElizaOS executable
   - Sets up environment variables needed by tests
   - Determines optimal command to run ElizaOS (`global` vs `local build`)

2. **Test Utilities**:

   - Logging functions (`log_info`, `log_error`, `log_warning`)
   - Test assertion functions (`assert_success`, `assert_failure`)
   - Output validation (`assert_stdout_contains`, `assert_stderr_contains`)
   - File and directory checks (`assert_file_exists`, `assert_dir_exists`)
   - Command execution wrapper (`run_elizaos`)

3. **Cleanup Functions**:
   - Trap-based cleanup to ensure test directories are removed
   - Project-specific cleanup for node_modules and other large artifacts

### Individual Test Scripts (`test_<command>.sh`)

Each test script follows a consistent structure:

1. **Setup Phase**:

   - Sources `setup_test_env.sh` for common utilities
   - Calls `prepare_test_environment` to create isolated test directory
   - Sets up test-specific variables and requirements

2. **Test Cases**:

   - Each test is clearly labeled with descriptive log messages
   - Commands are executed via `run_elizaos` wrapper
   - Results are validated with various assertion functions
   - Many tests track pass/fail counts for summary reporting

3. **Cleanup and Reporting**:
   - Cleans up test-specific artifacts
   - Reports test summary (total, passed, failed)
   - Returns appropriate exit code based on test results

## Test Workflow

When running the full test suite via `run_cli_tests.sh`:

1. **Dependencies** are verified (bun, node)
2. **Test server** is started in the background with isolated data directory
3. **Server availability** is confirmed via health check endpoint
4. **Test scripts** are executed sequentially with timeouts
5. **Results** are captured and summarized
6. **Cleanup** operations remove all temporary files and stop the server
7. **Exit code** indicates overall test suite success or failure

For individual test scripts:

1. **Test environment** is prepared via `prepare_test_environment`
2. **Test operations** are performed against either the global CLI or a local build
3. **Assertions** verify expected behavior and outputs
4. **Test-specific cleanup** removes artifacts before exit

## Test Character Assets

The `test-characters` directory contains JSON character definition files used by various tests:

- `ada.json`: Ada character configuration
- `max.json`: Max character configuration
- `shaw.json`: Shaw character configuration

These are loaded by the test server and used in agent-related tests.

## Adding New Tests

When adding new tests:

1. Create a new `test_<command>.sh` file based on an existing test
2. Include proper test tracking logic
3. Ensure idempotent execution (clean up after yourself)
4. Add comprehensive assertions
5. Update this README with details about the new tests

**Test Coverage Status: Up-to-date as of 2025-04-17**

_All tests use isolated temporary environments and have descriptive names for clarity._

This directory contains Bash test scripts that verify the functionality of the `elizaos` CLI commands in realistic user scenarios.

## Test Coverage

| Test File              | Command(s) Tested        | Test Cases Summary                                                                |
| ---------------------- | ------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `test_agent.bats`      | `elizaos agent`          | Help, list (default/API), start/stop agent, full agent lifecycle                  |
| `test_create.bats`     | `elizaos create`         | Help, create (project/plugin), duplicate/invalid names/types, deps                |
| `test_dev.bats`        | `elizaos dev`            | Help, dev server (port/background), server/API endpoints, agent commands, cleanup |
| `test_env.bats`        | `elizaos env`            | Help, list environment variables from server API                                  |
| `test_plugin.bats`     | `elizaos plugin`         | TBD (add summary when implemented)                                                |
| `test_project.bats`    | `elizaos project`        | Help, create/list projects, plugins, add plugin                                   |
| `test_publish.bats`    | `elizaos plugin publish` | Help, validate, pack, publish with auth, bump-version in plugin projects          |
| `test_start.bats`      | `elizaos start`          | Help, (add more tests as implemented)                                             |
| `test_test.bats`       | `elizaos test`           | Help, run simple test file, fail on error                                         |
| `test_update.bats`     | `elizaos update`         | TBD (add summary when implemented)                                                |
| `test_update-cli.bats` | `elizaos update-cli`     | TBD (add summary when implemented)                                                |

_Note: Plugin installation is now tested via `test_project.bats` using `elizaos project add-plugin`. If any test file is present but not yet implemented, its test cases are marked as 'TBD.'_.\_

---

## Setup & Running Tests

### Prerequisites

- **Node.js** (v18+ recommended)
- **bun** (for CLI and dependencies)
- **bats-core** ([Install](https://github.com/bats-core/bats-core) or `brew install bats-core`)
- **git** (for project/plugin tests)

### Install & Build

```bash
bun install
bun run build
```

### Running

- **All tests:**
  ```bash
  ./run_all_bats.sh
  ```
- **Single test:**
  ```bash
  bats test_agent.bats
  # or
  bats test_publish.bats
  ```

### Env Variables

- No special env vars needed. To override test server port, set `ELIZA_TEST_PORT`.

### Troubleshooting

- Ensure `bun`, `node`, and `bats-core` are installed and in `PATH`.
- Make scripts executable: `chmod +x *.bats *.sh`.
- If tests fail due to missing build, run `bun run build`.
- For port conflicts, set `ELIZA_TEST_PORT` to a free port.
- All tests run in isolated temp directories; avoid polluting the project root.

---

## How the Test Suite Works

The Eliza CLI test suite is designed for reliability, reproducibility, and easy debugging. Here’s how the full flow works:

1. **Test Runner Script (`run_all_bats.sh`)**

   - This script is the entry point for running all CLI tests.
   - It sets up the environment, verifies dependencies, and ensures a clean start.
   - It starts a dedicated test server (in the background) on a configurable port and waits for it to become available via a health check.
   - It then discovers and runs each `*.bats` test file in sequence, reporting progress and results for each.

2. **Test Script Execution**

   - Each test script runs in its own isolated temporary directory, created at the start and cleaned up at the end.
   - The test scripts set up any required environment variables and use helper functions for assertions and logging.
   - Each test case is named and described for clear reporting.
   - All commands (e.g., `elizaos create`, `elizaos agent`, etc.) are run as if by a real user, simulating actual CLI usage.

3. **Server and Asset Management**

   - The test server loads character assets from the `test-characters` directory for agent-related tests.
   - Server health is checked before tests begin to avoid race conditions.
   - Server is stopped and cleaned up after all tests complete.

4. **Result Collection and Reporting**

   - The runner script collects the results from each test file, showing a summary (total, passed, failed) at the end.
   - If any test fails, it is clearly reported, and the script exits with a non-zero status code.
   - Debug output is included for failed commands to aid troubleshooting.

5. **Cleanup**
   - All temporary directories and files created during the tests are removed.
   - The test server and any background processes are stopped.

### Key Features

- **Isolation:** Every test runs in a fresh temp directory, preventing pollution and ensuring repeatability.
- **Descriptive Output:** Test names and comments make it easy to see what is being tested and why a failure occurred.
- **Extensibility:** New test scripts can be added easily by following the existing structure.

## Adding New Tests

1. Copy an existing `test_<command>.bats`.
2. Use proper test tracking and cleanup logic.
3. Add comprehensive assertions.
4. Update the test coverage table above.

2. **Testing Framework**: The plugin test command still needs implementation2f:Ta39,## Pull Request: Add Livepeer LLM Inference Plugin to Eliza Framework

### Description
This PR introduces the **Livepeer LLM Inference Plugin** for the Eliza framework. It enables language-model inference via Livepeer’s API gateway, allowing agents to leverage a variety of models without requiring individual API keys.

---

### Features
- **LLM Inference Processing**  
  Seamless POST requests to Livepeer’s `/llm` endpoint for chat-style interactions.
- **Multi-Model Support**  
  Automatically selects from small or large models, or custom endpoints, based on configuration.
- **Zero-Key Integration**  
  No need for proprietary API keys—uses gateway defaults or runtime settings.

---

### Implementation Details
- **New Package**  
  Added `packages/plugin-livepeer-inference/` containing the TypeScript plugin and Zod schema.
- **Config Schema**  
  Validates and parses:  
  - `LIVEPEER_GATEWAY_URL`  
  - Optional model overrides (`LIVEPEER_MODEL`, `LIVEPEER_LARGE_MODEL`)  
  - Runtime overrides for `temperature` and `maxTokens`
- **Core Logic**  
  - `callLivepeerLLM()` helper builds messages and handles HTTP calls  
  - Exposes three hooks under `ModelType`:  
    - `TEXT_SMALL`  
    - `TEXT_LARGE`  
    - `TEXT_EMBEDDING` (character-code embedding fallback)

---

### Setup & Usage
1. **Install**  
   ```bash
   pnpm install packages/plugin-livepeer-inference
   ```
2. **Configure**  
   - Add your gateway URL in `.env` or runtime settings:  
     ```env
     LIVEPEER_GATEWAY_URL=https://your-gateway.livepeer.com
     LIVEPEER_MODEL=meta-llama/Meta-Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct
     ```
   - (Optional) Override temperature or max tokens:  
     ```env
     LIVEPEER_TEMPERATURE=0.7
     LIVEPEER_MAX_TOKENS=1024
     ```
3. **Enable**  
   Include `"plugin-livepeer-inference"` in your agent’s `plugins` array.

---

### Testing
- **Test Suite**:  
  - Core inference flows covered under `packages/plugin-livepeer-inference/tests/*`  
  - Zod schema validation tests for missing/invalid config
- **Integration**:  
  Verified against [[UD1sto/elizav2-livepeer](https://github.com/UD1sto/elizav2-livepeer)](https://github.com/UD1sto/elizav2-livepeer)
- **Known Issue**:  
  - Intermittent `503 Gateway` errors on reflection LLM calls from the bootstrap plugin. Under investigation; core flow still completes successfully.

---

### Checklist
- [x] Added TypeScript typings and Zod schema  
- [x] Wrote unit tests covering main code paths  
- [x] Verified end-to-end flow in demo repository  
- [ ] Resolve 503 error

# Background

## What does this PR do?

This PR implements a complete media API that allows:
- Uploading media attachments (images, documents, etc.)
- Retrieving media by ID
- Associating media with messages
- Retrieving all attachments for a specific message

The implementation uses both file system storage and runtime cache for redundancy, ensuring attachments remain available even if one storage method fails.

Features (non-breaking change which adds functionality)

     - Groups navigation

     - Connection status
     - Agent dashboard

     - Agent naming
     - System prompt
     - Configuration tabs
     - Save functionality

35:T56c,# Relates to

Adds instrumentation support to LLM plugins for better observability and debugging.

# Risks

**Medium** — Changes involve instrumentation code across plugin logic. Potential risks include:
- Performance overhead if not handled properly.
- Incorrect spans/tags leading to confusing traces.
- Integration issues with existing telemetry systems.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

This PR adds OpenTelemetry-based instrumentation to the LLM plugin modules. It introduces tracing for key functions to capture span data like plugin execution time, plugin name, and error logs if any failures occur.

## What kind of change is this?

- Improvements (misc. changes to existing features)
- Features (non-breaking change which adds functionality)

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

Start with the `instrumentation/` directory and review the changes to the plugin execution paths where spans have been added.

## Detailed testing steps

- Run the system with the environment variable to enable tracing.
- Execute multiple LLM plugin calls (successful and failing).
- Verify the traces are correctly emitted and visible in the backend (e.g., Jaeger, Zipkin).
- Confirm trace tags and span names are descriptive and accurate.

# Relates to

https://linear.app/eliza-labs/issue/ELI2-195/ui-replace-text-input-with-dropdown-for-voice-model-selection

# Risks

Low. The changes modify the voice model selection logic and plugin management, but use existing components and follow established patterns. The changes are isolated to the voice model selection flow and don't affect core functionality.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

This PR improves the voice model selection by enhancing how plugins are managed when changing between voice model providers. Specifically:

1. Makes voice model to plugin mapping configurable in voice-models.ts
2. Ensures only one voice-related plugin is active at a time (removes previous provider's plugin when switching)
3. Improves the UI to clearly indicate which plugins are required by the selected voice model
4. Prevents users from removing plugins that are required by the current voice model

## What kind of change is this?

Improvements (enhancement to existing voice model selection and plugin management features)

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

1. packages/client/src/config/voice-models.ts - Added configurable provider-to-plugin mapping
2. packages/client/src/components/character-form.tsx - Updated voice model selection logic
3. packages/client/src/components/plugins-panel.tsx - Enhanced UI for plugin management

## Detailed testing steps

1. Create a new character or edit an existing one
2. Select a local voice model (e.g., "Local Voice - Female 1")
   - Verify the Local AI plugin is automatically added
   - Verify the plugin appears with a green background in the plugins panel

3. Switch to an ElevenLabs voice model (e.g., "ElevenLabs - Rachel")
   - Verify the Local AI plugin is automatically removed
   - Verify the ElevenLabs plugin is automatically added
   - Verify only one voice-related plugin is present
   - Verify a toast notification appears about the plugin update

4. Try to remove the ElevenLabs plugin
   - Verify it can't be removed while the ElevenLabs voice model is selected
   - Verify a toast notification appears explaining why it can't be removed

5. Switch back to a local voice model
   - Verify the ElevenLabs plugin is removed and the Local AI plugin is added
   - Verify only one voice-related plugin is present

# Relates to
Closes elizaOS/eliza#42

# Risks
Medium — if the GAIMIN model endpoint changes format, text generation may break.

# Background
We want to add a new model provider (Gaimin) so Eliza can talk to the cloud.gaimin.io LLM API.  
See the full integration spec here:  
https://drive.google.com/file/d/1neYXA6xjMWUVqwxx5yRxHKdUVGwVq1jd/view?usp=sharing

## Overview
This document outlines the process of integrating the Gaimin Model Provider into the ElizaOS project. It provides a step-by-step guide, code examples, and key considerations to help developers quickly understand and implement the integration.

## Purpose and Scope
- **Purpose:** Demonstrate a baseline integration of the Gaimin Model Provider.  
- **Scope:** Provide example code snippets, configuration instructions, and a directory structure overview. Note that the implementation is a starting point and requires further customization and testing for production use.

# Prerequisites
## Environment Setup
Ensure you have the following prerequisites before proceeding:  
- API key for the Gaimin Model Provider.

# Key Considerations
## Testing and Optimization
The current implementation has not undergone comprehensive testing or performance optimization. Users should perform rigorous testing, including unit tests and integration tests, and refine the code as necessary before deploying it in production environments.

## Security Considerations
As this is a preliminary integration example, it has not been fully audited for security vulnerabilities. A thorough security review is recommended to ensure that the integration adheres to your organization’s security policies and industry best practices.

## Future Enhancements and Contributions
This baseline implementation is open for enhancements. Developers are encouraged to contribute improvements, including code optimization, additional features, and broader testing coverage, in line with the ElizaOS project’s contribution guidelines.

# What does this PR do?
- Adds `ModelProviderName.GAIMIN` to `packages/core/src/types.ts`
- Implements the GAIMIN fetch logic in `packages/core/src/generation.ts`
- Exposes the Gaimin models in `packages/core/src/models.ts`
- Updates agent key lookup in `agent/src/index.ts`
- Bundles a new `@ai-sdk/gaimin` package under `packages/gaimin`

## What kind of change is this?
**Features** (non-breaking): adds a new model provider.

# Documentation of changes
- ✔ External integration spec:  
  https://drive.google.com/file/d/1neYXA6xjMWUVqwxx5yRxHKdUVGwVq1jd/view?usp=sharing

# Testing
## Where should a reviewer start?

1. Create or update your `.env` with:
   ```bash
   GAIMIN_API_KEY=<your key>
   GAIMIN_API_URL=
  GAIMIN_MODEL="llama3.2:3b"
   ```
2. Run Eliza Character with `gaimin` specified as its model provider in the character.json file.

3b:T693,# Telegram Plugin Service Refactoring

## Relates to

- Telegram integration stability
- Forum topics support / rooms in ElizaOS system

## Risks

- Medium

## Background

The Telegram service implementation requires refactoring to better handle large groups and forum topics.
Previously, we only emitted the WORLD_JOINED event for connection which caused synchronization issues.
Now we sync Telegram directly by calling `ensureWorldExist` -> `syncTelegramWorld`, `ensureRoomExist` -> `syncTelegramRoom` and lastly `ensureConnection` methods.

## What does this PR do?

This PR refactors the Telegram service implementation to improve reliability, maintainability, and performance when handling Telegram chats, especially forum topics. The changes focus on optimizing middleware flow, standardizing entity synchronization, and properly managing chat discovery.

With emphasis on correctly handling synchronization. We also emit WORLD_JOINED event to make sure everything is synced properly.

## What kind of change is this?

- [x] Refactoring (non-breaking change) to be compatible with v2
- [x] Performance enhancement

## Documentation changes needed?

The code is well documented with JSDoc and the author has added detailed comments to many methods.

## Detailed testing steps

1. Start the agent with the Telegram plugin enabled
2. Add the bot to a group chat and verify messages can be sent/received
3. Test the onboarding when running the-org
4. Create a forum topic in a supergroup and verify room creation, message handling (agent should respond to the topic) 
5. Add new members to a group and verify they are synchronized properly

3c:Tbf6,# Fix Anthropic Plugin JSON Parsing for Responses with Code Blocks

## Risks

Low - This PR addresses an edge case in the JSON parsing logic for Anthropic responses containing code blocks. The changes improve the robustness of the existing error handling without changing the API.

## Background

### What does this PR do?

This PR fixes an issue in the Anthropic plugin where responses containing code blocks with language identifiers (like `bash`) are causing JSON parsing errors. The specific issue occurs when the model returns valid JSON containing markdown code blocks in string values, but the parser fails to handle the content correctly.

### What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking change which fixes an issue)

### Documentation changes needed?

No documentation changes are required as this fixes an internal parsing mechanism.

## Testing

### Where should a reviewer start?

The reviewer should start by examining the `extractAndParseJSON` function in `packages/plugin-anthropic/src/index.ts` to understand how the JSON parsing has been improved.

### Detailed testing steps

1. Test sending messages that would result in responses with code blocks
2. Verify that responses with bash/code blocks are correctly parsed
3. Verify that the error "Invalid JSON returned from Anthropic model" no longer occurs for valid responses with code blocks

## Screenshots

### Before

Error when receiving a response with code blocks:

```
[2025-04-08 13:06:47] ERROR: Failed to parse JSON from Anthropic response:
    message: "(SyntaxError) JSON Parse error: Unexpected identifier \"bash\""
[2025-04-08 13:06:47] ERROR: Raw response: {
    "thought": "I'll help them get started with ElizaOS by providing clear installation instructions from the documentation",
    "message": "I'll help you get started with ElizaOS! The quickest way is to install the CLI tool globally:\n\n1. Install the CLI:\nbash\nnpm install -g @elizaos/cli@beta\n\n\n2. Create a new project:\nbash\nelizaos create\n\n\n3. Start your project:\nbash\ncd your-project\nelizaos start\n\n\nOnce started, you can visit https://localhost:3000 to interact with your agent through the web interface. Let me know if you need help with any specific part of the setup!"
}
```

### After

The same response is now correctly parsed without errors, allowing the agent to properly display messages with code blocks.

## Discord username

<!-- Add your Discord username here -->3e:T5ae,# CLI Update Improvements

This PR enhances the ElizaOS CLI update and plugin installation mechanisms with more robust functionality:

## What's Changed

### Package Management System Improvements
- Added a centralized `executeInstallation()` function to standardize package installation across the codebase
- Added detection for npx/bunx running environments to prevent unnecessary installations
- Improved version and tag handling for package installations

### CLI Update Command Enhancements
- Refactored `update-cli` to use the new centralized installation system
- Added proper validation to prevent updates when running via npx/bunx
- Extracted core update logic to a separate `performCliUpdate()` function
- Improved error handling and logging
- Added better version compatibility checking

### Plugin Installation System Overhaul
- Completely reworked plugin installation with a more reliable multi-attempt strategy:
  1. Try local installation first (preferred)
  2. Fall back to CLI directory installation if running globally
  3. Use global installation as a last resort
- Added verification steps to confirm successful plugin imports after installation
- Improved handling of repository URLs and version strings
- Better error reporting during installation attempts

These changes significantly improve the reliability of the CLI update and plugin installation processes while maintaining backward compatibility.

# Risks

# Background
This PR improves documentation clarity by distinguishing between:
- **Framework users** who want to build agents (using `elizaos start` / CLI tools)
- **Core contributors** who want to develop ElizaOS itself (using the monorepo)

Confusion previously existed because the docs and contributing guide mixed both paths. These updates make the difference immediately clear to new users.

# What does this PR do?
- Updates `CONTRIBUTING.md` to outline two paths clearly.
- Updates `intro.md` with a "Choose Your Path" section right after the intro.
- Updates `quickstart.md` to split instructions cleanly for users vs contributors.
- Updates `docs/docs/contributing.md` to explain how contributions differ between users and contributors.

# What kind of change is this?
- Documentation improvement (non-breaking)

# Documentation changes needed?
- My changes **have updated the project documentation** accordingly.

# Testing
- Verified locally that modified docs render properly.
- Confirmed clear separation between framework users and contributors.
- No functional changes, so no code-level testing needed.

# Where should a reviewer start?
- `eliza/CONTRIBUTING.md`
- `eliza/docs/docs/intro.md`
- `eliza/docs/docs/quickstart.md`
- `eliza/docs/docs/contributing.md`

# Background

44:T5c4,# Enhanced Command-Line Handling for create-eliza

This PR improves the user experience of the create-eliza CLI tool with comprehensive flag handling and path detection:

## 1. Robust Flag and Argument Processing
* Properly handles all supported flags: `-d/--dir`, `-t/--type`, and `-y/--yes`
* Ensures flags are correctly passed to the underlying CLI regardless of position
* Preserves flag functionality when using both npm and npx command forms

## 2. Intelligent Directory Path Detection
* Added smart path detection to recognize various directory path formats
* Now correctly handles directory names with or without path separators
* Supports multiple command syntax patterns:
 * Standard flag usage: `npm create eliza -d ./my-dir`
 * Auto-detected paths: `npm create eliza ./my-dir` or `npm create eliza my-project-dir`
 * Combined with type: `npm create eliza plugin ./plugins-dir/my-plugin`

## 3. Consistent Help Command Experience
* Added a dedicated help command that works with npm: `npm create eliza help`
* Help output matches exactly what users see with `npx elizaos create --help`
* Welcome message only appears when creating projects, not when viewing help

These changes standardize the CLI experience across npm and npx command forms, making it more intuitive and aligned with user expectations. Any command that works with `npx elizaos create` now works consistently with `npm create eliza`, providing a seamless developer experience.45:T15a9,Bumps the pip group with 2 updates in the /scripts/bug_hunt directory: [cryptography](https://github.com/pyca/cryptography) and [h11](https://github.com/python-hyper/h11).

- `@dependabot merge` will merge this PR after your CI passes on it
- `@dependabot squash and merge` will squash and merge this PR after your CI passes on it
- `@dependabot cancel merge` will cancel a previously requested merge and block automerging
- `@dependabot reopen` will reopen this PR if it is closed
- `@dependabot close` will close this PR and stop Dependabot recreating it. You can achieve the same result by closing it manually
- `@dependabot show <dependency name> ignore conditions` will show all of the ignore conditions of the specified dependency
- `@dependabot ignore <dependency name> major version` will close this group update PR and stop Dependabot creating any more for the specific dependency's major version (unless you unignore this specific dependency's major version or upgrade to it yourself)
- `@dependabot ignore <dependency name> minor version` will close this group update PR and stop Dependabot creating any more for the specific dependency's minor version (unless you unignore this specific dependency's minor version or upgrade to it yourself)
- `@dependabot ignore <dependency name>` will close this group update PR and stop Dependabot creating any more for the specific dependency (unless you unignore this specific dependency or upgrade to it yourself)
- `@dependabot unignore <dependency name>` will remove all of the ignore conditions of the specified dependency
- `@dependabot unignore <dependency name> <ignore condition>` will remove the ignore condition of the specified dependency and ignore conditions

<!-- end of auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai -->4a:T540,# Fix Plugin Loading in Global CLI Installations

## Problem
When the ElizaOS CLI is installed globally, it fails to load required plugins with errors like:
```
Cannot find package '@elizaos/plugin-sql' imported from /usr/local/lib/node_modules/@elizaos/cli/dist/chunk-Q5F6LCOL.js
```

This occurs because the CLI tries to install plugins locally while running from a global installation.

## Solution
This PR adds package manager detection to ensure plugins are installed in the same context as the CLI:

1. Created a new utility module `package-manager.ts` to:
   - Detect if running globally vs locally
   - Identify which package manager is being used (npm/npx or bun/bunx)
   - Generate appropriate install commands

2. Updated `install-plugin.ts` to:
   - Use the `-g` flag when installing plugins for a global CLI
   - Support both npm and bun package managers

3. Updated `update-cli.ts` to:
   - Use the same package manager detection
   - Ensure consistent update behavior

## Testing
- Verified plugin loading works when CLI is installed globally
- Tested with both npm/npx and bun/bunx

## Impact
- Fixes agent initialization errors related to missing plugins
- Improves developer experience by eliminating manual plugin installation
- Ensures consistent behavior across different package managers

4c:T58a,# Relates to

[ELI2-189: UI Bug - Advanced Agent Creation Fails with TypeError (Same as Basic)](https://linear.app/eliza-labs/issue/ELI2-189/ui-bug-advanced-agent-creation-fails-with-typeerror-same-as-basic)

# Risks

Low - This fix addresses a specific issue with agent initialization where default plugins are not being properly included. The changes are focused on ensuring that required plugins are always included during agent initialization, which should not affect other functionality.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

This PR fixes an issue where agent creation fails with a TypeError when using advanced configuration options. The error occurs because the agent is initialized without the required default plugins, specifically the SQL plugin. The fix ensures that the default plugins (`@elizaos/plugin-sql` and `@elizaos/plugin-local-ai`) are always included during agent initialization.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fixes (non-breaking change which fixes an issue)

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation.
